title: "kubernetes_core_pod_template"
description: "`kubernetes_core_pod_template` is a real, reusable pod specification embedded inside a higher-level controller -- a Deployment..."

`kubernetes_core_pod_template` is a real, reusable pod specification embedded inside a higher-level controller -- a Deployment, a Job -- the actual blueprint the controller stamps out real pods from.

Real, typed bindings generated directly from the real Kubernetes OpenAPI specification, in every SDK language -- every tab below is a complete, runnable program, not a fragment, real enough to save and run exactly as shown.

## Example

<Tabs>
  <Tab title="Go">
    ```go
    // ubx sdk gen --only kubernetes --lang go --out ./local-sdk
    // go.mod: replace github.com/ubiquex/ubx-sdk-kubernetes/sdk/go => ./local-sdk/kubernetes/sdk/go
    package main

    import (
    	ubx "github.com/ubiquex/ubx-sdk-go/runtime"
    	core "github.com/ubiquex/ubx-sdk-kubernetes/sdk/go/kubernetes/core"
    )

    func main() {
    	ubx.Main(ubx.Stack("payments", func() {
    		ubx.Intent(ubx.IntentInfo{Summary: "payments own pod template"})

    		ubx.Resource(core.PodTemplate, "ci-runner", core.PodTemplateConfig{
    			Name:      "ci-runner",
    			Namespace: "example",
    		})
    	}))
    }
    ```
  </Tab>

    ```typescript
    export default stack("payments", () => {
      intent({ summary: "payments own pod template" });

      resource(PodTemplate, "ci-runner", {
        name: "ci-runner",
        namespace: "example",
      });
    });
    ```

  <Tab title="Python">
    ```python
    # ubx sdk gen --only kubernetes --lang py --out ./local-sdk
    # export PYTHONPATH=./local-sdk/kubernetes/sdk/python:$PYTHONPATH
    import ubx_sdk as ubx
    from ubx.kubernetes.core import PodTemplate, PodTemplateConfig

    def describe():
        ubx.intent("payments own pod template")

        ubx.resource(PodTemplate, "ci-runner", PodTemplateConfig(
            name="ci-runner",
            namespace="example",
        ))

    if __name__ == "__main__":
        ubx.run("payments", describe)
    ```

    <ResponseField name="spec" type="object">
      PodSpec is a description of a pod. Optional.
      <Expandable title="properties">
        <ResponseField name="active_deadline_seconds" type="number">
          Optional duration in seconds the pod may be active on the node relative to StartTime before the system will actively try to mark it failed and kill associated containers. Value must be a positive integer. Optional.
        </ResponseField>
        <ResponseField name="affinity" type="object">
          Affinity is a group of affinity scheduling rules. Optional.
          <Expandable title="properties">
            <ResponseField name="node_affinity" type="object">
              Node affinity is a group of node affinity scheduling rules. Optional.
              <Expandable title="properties">
                <ResponseField name="preferred_during_scheduling_ignored_during_execution" type="list(object)">
                  The scheduler will prefer to schedule pods to nodes that satisfy the affinity expressions specified by this field, but it may choose a node that violates one or more of the expressions. The node that is most preferred is the one with the greatest sum of weights, i.e. for each node that meets all of the scheduling requirements (resource request, requiredDuringScheduling affinity expressions, etc.), compute a sum by iterating through the elements of this field and adding "weight" to the sum if the node matches the corresponding matchExpressions; the node(s) with the highest sum are the most preferred. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="preference" type="object">
                      The node selector term that defines the preferred node selection criteria. It contains match_expressions and match_fields to specify node label and field requirements. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                      <Expandable title="properties">
                        <ResponseField name="match_expressions" type="list(object)">
                          A list of node selector requirements that specify the node labels that must be met for the preference. Each requirement consists of a key, an operator (e.g., In, NotIn, Exists, DoesNotExist), and a list of values. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                          <Expandable title="properties">
                            <ResponseField name="key" type="string">
                              The label key that the selector operates on (e.g., `kubernetes.io/hostname`). Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                            </ResponseField>
                            <ResponseField name="operator" type="string">
                              Defines the operator for the match expression, such as In, NotIn, Exists, DoesNotExist, Gt, or Lt. It specifies the relationship between the label key and the specified values. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                            </ResponseField>
                            <ResponseField name="values" type="list(string)">
                              Values is a list of string values for the match expression. It is required when the operator is In or NotIn, and must be empty when the operator is Exists or DoesNotExist. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                            </ResponseField>
                          </Expandable>
                        </ResponseField>
                        <ResponseField name="match_fields" type="list(object)">
                          match_fields is a list of node selector requirements that match nodes by their fields (e.g., metadata.name). Each requirement specifies a key, an operator (such as In, NotIn, Exists, DoesNotExist), and optionally a list of values. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                          <Expandable title="properties">
                            <ResponseField name="key" type="string">
                              The node field key that this requirement matches on, such as metadata.name. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                            </ResponseField>
                            <ResponseField name="operator" type="string">
                              The operator used to compare the node field against the specified values. Valid values are 'In', 'NotIn', 'Exists', 'DoesNotExist', 'Gt', and 'Lt'. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                            </ResponseField>
                            <ResponseField name="values" type="list(string)">
                              An array of string values used in the node affinity match field requirement. Must be non-empty for operators 'In' and 'NotIn', and must be empty for operators 'Exists' and 'DoesNotExist'. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                            </ResponseField>
                          </Expandable>
                        </ResponseField>
                      </Expandable>
                    </ResponseField>
                    <ResponseField name="weight" type="number">
                      Weight associated with the node affinity preference, ranging from 1 to 100. The scheduler uses this weight to calculate the sum of weights of matching node selectors to prioritize nodes. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                    </ResponseField>
                  </Expandable>
                </ResponseField>
                <ResponseField name="required_during_scheduling_ignored_during_execution" type="object">
                  A node selector represents the union of the results of one or more label queries over a set of nodes; that is, it represents the OR of the selectors represented by the node selector terms. Optional.
                  <Expandable title="properties">
                    <ResponseField name="node_selector_terms" type="list(object)" required>
                      Required. A list of node selector terms. The terms are ORed. Required.
                      <Expandable title="properties">
                        <ResponseField name="match_expressions" type="list(object)">
                          List of node selector requirements that specify label-based conditions for node selection. Each requirement includes a key, an operator (e.g., In, NotIn, Exists, DoesNotExist), and optionally a set of values. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                          <Expandable title="properties">
                            <ResponseField name="key" type="string">
                              The node label key that the selector matches against. For example, 'disktype' or 'kubernetes.io/hostname'. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                            </ResponseField>
                            <ResponseField name="operator" type="string">
                              The operator represents the relationship between the key and a set of values. Allowed values: In, NotIn, Exists, DoesNotExist, Gt, Lt. For In and NotIn, values must be provided; Exists and DoesNotExist check key presence; Gt and Lt compare integer values against the key's value. Optional; if omitted, computed by Kubernetes. **(AI-inferred)**
                            </ResponseField>
                            <ResponseField name="values" type="list(string)">

The responders are selected sequentially, according to their specified priority. If this field is not updated within the heartbeat deadline defined by the Eviction API (currently 20 minutes), the eviction is passed over to the next responder with a lower priority. If there is no other responder, the last default imperative-eviction.k8s.io/evictor responder with a priority of 100 will evict the pod using the imperative Eviction API (pods/&lt;name&gt;/eviction subresource). Optional.

              seLinuxChangePolicy defines how the container's SELinux label is applied to all volumes used by the Pod. It has no effect on nodes that do not support SELinux or to volumes does not support SELinux. Valid values are "MountOption" and "Recursive". "Recursive" means relabeling of all files on all Pod volumes by the container runtime. This may be slow for large volumes, but allows mixing privileged and unprivileged Pods sharing the same volume on the same node. "MountOption" mounts all eligible Pod volumes with `-o context` mount option. This requires all Pods that share the same volume to use the same SELinux label. It is not possible to share the same volume among privileged and unprivileged Pods. Eligible volumes are in-tree FibreChannel and iSCSI volumes, and all CSI volumes whose CSI driver announces SELinux support by setting spec.seLinuxMount: true in their CSIDriver instance. Other volumes are always re-labelled recursively. If not specified, "MountOption" is used. This field affects only Pods that have SELinux label set, either in PodSecurityContext or in SecurityContext of all containers. All Pods that use the same volume should use the same seLinuxChangePolicy, otherwise some pods can get stuck in ContainerCreating state.                   The scheduler will prefer to schedule pods to nodes that satisfy the affinity expressions specified by this field, but it may choose a node that violates one or more of the expressions. The node that is most preferred is the one with the greatest sum of weights, i.e. for each node that meets all of the scheduling requirements (resource request, requiredDuringScheduling affinity expressions, etc.), compute a sum by iterating through the elements of this field and adding "weight" to the sum if the node matches the corresponding matchExpressions; the node(s) with the highest sum are the most preferred. The responders are selected sequentially, according to their specified priority. If this field is not updated within the heartbeat deadline defined by the Eviction API (currently 20 minutes), the eviction is passed over to the next responder with a lower priority. If there is no other responder, the last default imperative-eviction.k8s.io/evictor responder with a priority of 100 will evict the pod using the imperative Eviction API (pods/&lt;name&gt;/eviction subresource). The maximum length of the responders list is 10. Responders are not supported when the pod is part of a PodGroup (.spec.schedulingGroup is set). This field can only be set on creation and is immutable afterwards. Optional.

Eligible volumes are in-tree FibreChannel and iSCSI volumes, and all CSI volumes whose CSI driver announces SELinux support by setting spec.seLinuxMount: true in their CSIDriver instance.
